annotations\n\nfrom persistence import Database\nfrom persistence.schema import BookRow\n\n\nclass BookRejected(ValueError):\n    \"\"\"A submission the application refuses to store; ``reason`` says why and ``field`` names the input.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, reason: str, field: str) -> None:\n        super().__init__(reason)\n        self.reason = reason\n        self.field = field\n\n\nclass BookNotFound(LookupError):\n    \"\"\"A removal named an id that no stored book carries; ``book_id`` is that id.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, book_id: int) -> None:\n        super().__init__(book_id)\n        self.book_id = book_id\n\n\ndef add_book(database: Database, title: str, author: str) -> BookRow:\n    \"\"\"Store a book appended after every existing book and return the stored row.\n\n    Values are expected already trimmed by the caller. An empty title or author is refused\n    without touching the store, so a stored book always has both.\n    \"\"\"\n    if not title:\n        raise BookRejected(\"rejected: title is empty\", field=\"title\")\n    if not author:\n        raise BookRejected(\"rejected: author is empty\", field=\"author\")\n    return database.books.create(title=title, author=author)\n\n\ndef list_books(database: Database) -> list[BookRow]:\n    \"\"\"Return every stored book in the order added, oldest first.\n\n    The order is enforced here, by ascending id, rather than trusted to the store's select, which\n    the generated persistence layer issues without an ORDER BY clause.\n    \"\"\"\n    return sorted(database.books.list(), key=lambda row: row.id)\n\n\ndef remove_book(database: Database, book_id: int) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Delete the stored book with ``book_id``; the remaining books keep their order.\n\n    An id that no stored book carries is refused with ``BookNotFound`` before any write, so an\n    unknown id never changes stored data.\n    \"\"\"\n    if database.books.get(id=book_id) is None:\n        raise BookNotFound(book_id)\n    database.books.delete(id=book_id)\n=== bin/run.sh\n#!/bin/sh\n# ReadingList development server launcher.\n# Activates the project .venv and starts Flask with the application factory.\nset -eu\n\nPROJECT_DIR=\"$(cd \"$(dirname \"$0\")/..\" && pwd)\"\ncd \"$PROJECT_DIR\"\n\nif [ ! -f .venv/bin/activate ]; then\n    echo \"error: .venv is missing. does not connect to it; connections are made on first use, per thread.\n    \"\"\"\n    settings = Settings.from_environment()\n    app = Flask(__name__, template_folder=str(TEMPLATE_FOLDER))\n add a book, list stored books in the order added, and remove a book.\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom flask import Blueprint, abort, current_app, jsonify, redirect, render_template, request\nfrom werkzeug.wrappers import Response\n\nfrom persistence import Database\n\nfrom .book_service import BookNotFound, BookRejected, add_book, list_books, remove_book\n\nbp = Blueprint(\"books\", __name__)\n\n\ndef _database() -> Database:\n    return current_app.extensions[\"database\"]\n\n\ndef _reading_list(error: str | None = None) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Render the reading list page: the alert region, the add form, and the books in the order added.\"\"\"\n    return render_template(\"reading_list.html\", books=list_books(_database()), error=error)\n\n\n@bp.get(\"/\")\ndef index() -> str:\n    \"\"\"Render the reading list page with every stored book in the order added.\"\"\"\n    return _reading_list()\n\n\n@bp.get(\"/books\")\ndef get_books() -> Response:\n    \"\"\"Return every stored book as a JSON array of ``id``, ``title`` and ``author``, oldest first.\"\"\"\n    return jsonify(\n        [{\"id\": row.id, \"title\": row.title, \"author\": row.author} for row in list_books(_database())]\n    )\n\n\n@bp.post(\"/books\")\ndef create_book() -> Response:\n    \"\"\"Add a book from the ``title`` and ``author`` form fields, then redirect to the start page.\n\n    A refused submission answers 400: as JSON with ``error`` and ``field`` when the client asks\n    for ``application/json``, otherwise as the reading list page with the reason in the alert region.\n    \"\"\"\n    title = request.form.get(\"title\", \"\").strip()\n    author = request.form.get(\"author\", \"\").strip()\n    try:\n        add_book(_database(), title=title, author=author)\n    except BookRejected as rejected:\n        if request.accept_mimetypes.best == \"application/json\":\n            refused = jsonify(error=rejected.reason, field=rejected.field)\n            refused.status_code = 400\n            return refused\n        return Response(\n            _reading_list(error=rejected.reason),\n            status=400,\n            mimetype=\"text/html\",\n        )\n    return redirect(\"/\", code=303)\n\n\n@bp.post(\"/books/<int:book_id>/delete\")\ndef delete_book(book_id: int) -> Response:\n    \"\"\"Remove the book with ``book_id``, then redirect to the start page; an unknown id answers 404.\"\"\"\n    try:\n        remove_book(_database(), book_id)\n    except BookNotFound:\n        abort(404)\n    return redirect(\"/\", code=303)\n=== app_server/book_service.py\n\"\"\"Book behavior for the ReadingList web application, free of any Flask dependency.\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom persistence import Database\nfrom persistence.schema import BookRow\n\n\nclass BookRejected(ValueError):\n    \"\"\"A submission the application refuses to store; ``reason`` says why and ``field`` names the input.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, reason: str, field: str) -> None:\n        super().__init__(reason)\n        self.reason = reason\n        self.field = field\n\n\nclass BookNotFound(LookupError):\n    \"\"\"A removal named an id that no stored book carries; ``book_id`` is that id.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, book_id: int) -> None:\n        super().__init__(book_id)\n        self.book_id = book_id\n\n\ndef add_book(database: Database, title: str, author: str) -> BookRow:\n    \"\"\"Store a book appended after every existing book and return the stored row.\n\n    Values are expected already trimmed by the caller. An empty title or author is refused\n    without touching the store, so a stored book always has both.\n    \"\"\"\n    if not title:\n        raise BookRejected(\"rejected: title is empty\", field=\"title\")\n    if not author:\n        raise BookRejected(\"rejected: author is empty\", field=\"author\")\n    return database.books.create(title=title, author=author)\n\n\ndef list_books(database: Database) -> list[BookRow]:\n    \"\"\"Return every stored book in the order added, oldest first.\n\n    The order is enforced here, by ascending id, rather than trusted to the store's select, which\n    the generated persistence layer issues without an ORDER BY clause.\n    \"\"\"\n    return sorted(database.books.list(), key=lambda row: row.id)\n\n\ndef remove_book(database: Database, book_id: int) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Delete the stored book with ``book_id``; the remaining books keep their order.\n\n    An id that no stored book carries is refused with ``BookNotFound`` before any write, so an\n    unknown id never changes stored data.\n    \"\"\"\n    if database.books.get(id=book_id) is None:\n        raise BookNotFound(book_id)\n    database.books.delete(id=book_id)\n=== bin/run.sh\n#!/bin/sh\n# ReadingList development server launcher.\n# Activates the project .venv and starts Flask with the application factory.\nset -eu\n\nPROJECT_DIR=\"$(cd \"$(dirname \"$0\")/..\" && pwd)\"\ncd \"$PROJECT_DIR\"\n\nif [ ! -f .venv/bin/activate ]; then\n    echo \"error: .venv is missing.
